# AutomataFramework: master delivery plan

> **Direction (Owner, 2026-10-11):** Ruken first; core from FloorFifty, The Last Light and Ruken, everything else as extensions; PixiJS for 2D and 3D; Effect outside the sim, Koota inside; CI/CD, deployment, mobile UI and the content compiler early. Read [03 Continuation](03-continuation-prompt.md) before choosing work.
>
> **Owner, 2026-10-30** (in the session's chat; [AGENTS.md](../AGENTS.md)): plan to completion, the website included ("pixi drops in 2 weeks, so proceed as if we can go to completion, I want everything done including website"); the AF 1.0 launch waits for Pixi 3D's public release (about 2026-11-13) and builds on the vendored `@pixi/3d` 0.1.0-beta.2 meanwhile ("beta.2 is the latest, vendor it", #188). The agent merges its own PRs once every check, parity included, is green on the head ("you can merge PR"). "No more orchestrator, we're only working on AF": there is no hub or bus, and questions, device-test requests and acceptances go to the Owner in the session. FigureBuilder (`mcthaydt/FigureBuilder` `a1f7246`) is the procedural figure source; `games/base-3d` uses core only and the M6 extensions are built in Plus; other repositories are not edited (AF-side patches); the game server ships as a package and deploy target, with staging on a local VM; `@typeonce/ritmo-tools` goes in where it fits (ASSET). The eleven decisions and the agent defaults open to veto are in [03](03-continuation-prompt.md).

## How to use this plan

This is the only task list. Every piece of work has a task ID; if it is not here, nobody builds it. Read [00 Overview](00-overview.md) and [01 Decomposition](01-decomposition.md) first. Dated specs own contracts; this plan owns order. A row here is not an executable TDD plan.

Each milestone starts with its spec (`docs/superpowers/specs/YYYY-MM-DD-<slug>-design.md`), reviewed by the Owner; that review freezes the milestone's contracts, after which changes are additive or go through a written change request. Each task then gets a just-in-time TDD plan in `docs/superpowers/plans/` before code. A row is ticked only with its evidence recorded in the execution record below. Gates are reported separately: implemented, locally verified, CI verified, deployed, Owner accepted.

## DX Small games, agent-native, website claims made true

Owner, 2026-10-16: the framework must match what the website sells: small changes are small diffs, and agents get AF's rules, real API and checks from AF. The [DX spec](superpowers/specs/2026-10-16-dx-small-games-ai-native-design.md) maps every row of the website's restoration ledger (`automata-website` `docs/2026-10-14-cut-claims.md`) to a task, an evidence step, an Owner decision or a permanent retirement. | [x] | DX.2c — Follow-up (found in DX.2) — Dispose the default screens' sim on runtime shutdown | Lanes **runtime**, **game**. The default screens dispose the sim when the title is entered. `RuntimeHandle.shutdown()` closes the play or paused screen, but no screen hook tells a scope closed by shutdown from one closed by a transition. So a sim alive at shutdown is left for garbage collection, without `Sim.dispose()`. This needs a runtime-scoped finalizer for game state, or a shutdown flag on `ScreenContext`. **2026-10-30:** not started; a lane is building it. Left: a runtime-scoped finalizer for game state, or a shutdown flag on `ScreenContext`, so a live sim gets `Sim.dispose()` on shutdown (`RuntimeHandle.shutdown` is `packages/runtime/src/boot/run-game.ts:159`); failing tests first in `packages/game` and `runtime`; the API report. Size M. **Merged 2026-10-30:** PR #208 (`46d203c`); CI green on the head, parity included. |

| [x] | DX.3b — Follow-up (found in DX.3) — render-pixi stage hooks and pixel-art resolution | Lane **render**; Owner review. (1) `PixiRendererOptions.setup` may return `{ apply, draw, reset }` hooks (DX.3 decision E-1, DX §3.4 amendment; approved by the Owner, 2026-10-16). (2) render-pixi draws the letterboxed canvas at the device's resolution (capped at 2), so a 320×192 pixel-art game fills ~1.5 M device pixels a frame at Pixel 7. In headless software GL that measured ~159 ms a frame against ~35 ms at 330×200, and Emberwick's clock-driven golden journey needed a 300 s timeout (E-7). An option to draw pixel-art games at their logical size and upscale would cut that. **Done (verified 2026-10-30):** (1) the stage hooks merged with DX.3 (#63, `36f82d3`): `PixiRendererOptions.setup` may return `{ apply, draw, reset }` (`renderer.ts`, `renderer.test.ts`); (2) M4.9 (#78, `50f1afa`) draws the world and UI at the logical size into `CompositeTarget` and nearest-upscales (`letterbox/composite-target.ts` and its test), on by default with `pixelArt`, which `defineGame` sets. A cleanup, not a gate: re-measure the phone golden journey and, if it is now fast, drop the 300 s timeout at `games/emberwick/e2e/base-2d.e2e.ts:146` and its stale "device resolution" comment. |

DX.6 follow-ups (found 2026-10-16, not started):

- **`style` step in `automata check`.** PRODUCT F3 says the check runs style (role suffixes, kebab-case), but `tooling/check-style` is `private` with no bin, so a game cannot run it. Publish it, or fold it into `@mcthaydt/automata-check-boundaries`, then add the step. Until then DX spec §3.6 and PRODUCT F3 disagree.
- **koota SoA code generation.** koota 0.6.6 builds SoA trait accessors with `Function(...)`. AF's `defineComponent` makes AoS traits, so the no-eval scan allows them. A game that calls koota's `trait({ ... })` directly would pass the scan, and only `--csp` catches it, and only if the trait is created at boot. Options: a `check-boundaries` rule against object-schema `trait()` calls in a sim, or a request to koota for a no-eval path.
- **EX.2 path.** When `examples/base-2d` moves to `games/emberwick`, update `packages/cli`'s `check:example` script, the one place that path appears.

DX.7 follow-ups (found 2026-10-16, not started):

- **Release version bump.** `pnpm agent-skills` fails once `release:version` bumps the packages, until `pnpm agent-skills --write` copies the new `api.md` and sets the plugin version. Add it to `release:version` after a build and `pnpm api-index` (ship lane).
- **EX.2 path, again.** `examples/base-2d` now also appears in `tooling/agent-skills/src/install.test.ts` (the hook's real-game run), and its `@mcthaydt/automata-cli` dev dependency moves with it to `games/emberwick`.
- **The view step and `startGame`.** `automata-feature` describes the view in prose and the `automata` skill names today's folders; when DX.2 and DX.2b land, add a `rect`/`sprite` view to the example and update both skills (DX.3 or DX.5).
- **`test:install` in CI.** The install suite needs the `claude` CLI and the network; run it on af-linux when that runner takes jobs.

## EX One-file examples, like raylib

Owner, 2026-10-16: each example is one file, like raylib's. The [EX spec](superpowers/specs/2026-10-16-examples-one-file-design.md) replaces today's demos, which are whole Vite apps of 9–30 files built on Emberwick. Each example becomes `examples/<module>/<module>-<name>.ts`: 100 code lines or fewer, `export default defineGame(...)`, a raylib header, one runner, and parametric catalog, smoke and parity tests. Emberwick moves to `games/emberwick` (EX-D1). The examples are written first, as DX.2's and DX.2b's acceptance tests (EX-D2). TDD plan: [`2026-10-16-examples-one-file.md`](superpowers/plans/2026-10-16-examples-one-file.md).

The owner of the content plugin decides which. **2026-10-30:** not started; a lane is building it; size S; before DX.5. Only `tooling/examples-runner/src/vite-plugin.ts:86` sets `assetsInlineLimit: 0` (#142). Left (the agent's default for the content owner's choice): the content Vite plugin sets `build.assetsInlineLimit: 0`, and `automata check` reports `data:` URLs in `dist` that `connect-src 'self'` would refuse; red first with an asset under 4 kB. **Merged 2026-10-30:** PR #194 (`2a8ac8b`); CI green on the head, parity included. |

## PRODUCT Free core, Automata Plus, FloorFifty as the measure

Owner, 2026-10-16: someone can build FloorFifty (content excluded) on AF. Core is free and is a foundation that scales, and localization is part of it. Automata Plus is paid: every extension, copy-in UI and screen kits, and starter games. The [PRODUCT spec](superpowers/specs/2026-10-16-product-core-and-plus-design.md) has the FloorFifty gap ledger (at `ac72660`), the tiers, the cuts and the raylib API rules.

**Order (PD-3):** the foundation first (PRODUCT.1, PRODUCT.L, the core parts of M4 and M5, PRODUCT.7), then the Plus extensions on the FloorFifty path, then PRODUCT.8.

| [ ] | EX.4f — Follow-up (found in EX.4) — A screen-space filter verb for the umbrella | Lanes **render**, **game**; Owner review (new public API). EX spec §6 maps the old `renderer-2d` demo to `render/render-interpolation.ts` and `render/render-filter.ts`. A filter is a Pixi `Filter` built from a GLSL and WGSL pair (`filterPrograms`), and neither `Filter` nor `filterPrograms` is reachable through `@mcthaydt/automata`, the only import an example may use (EX spec §2), so `render-filter` cannot be written and the budget is not raised. Add a verb that takes the pair and a name and registers it in the stage's post-filter slot (best guess: `postFilter(name, { glsl, wgsl })` in `render-pixi`, exported by `game` and the umbrella, pinned in `packages/automata/src/index.test.ts`), then write `render-filter` on it. The old demo's filter was an identity pass-through, so no behavior is lost meanwhile: the post-filter slot and `filterPrograms` keep their render-pixi unit and three-engine parity tests. **Progress (2026-10-23):** the verb is drafted, implemented and locally checked in [the plan](superpowers/plans/2026-10-23-post-filter-verb.md) (the hub approved the shape on the bus): `postFilter(name, { glsl, wgsl, order?, minTier? })` and `PixiRendererOptions.postFilters` in `render-pixi`, re-exported by `game` and the umbrella; a filter that cannot be built is dropped with `render.filter.dropped`. Uniforms are deferred until a filter needs them (`filterPrograms` stays for extensions). `render/render-filter.ts` is written and checked locally on top ([plan](superpowers/plans/2026-10-23-ex4-render-filter.md)): two filters (`dusk`, and `scanlines` with `order: 1` and `minTier: 'medium'`), the tier keys, and a HUD that lists the filters running. Both PRs have merged (#111 `e2a3fbf`, #125 `c522734`) with hosted checks, parity and game-ci green; this row stays open for the Owner's review of the new public API. **Build done; the Owner's gate is open (2026-10-30):** #111 (`e2a3fbf`) and #125 (`c522734`) are merged; the row ticks after the Owner reviews `postFilter(name, { glsl, wgsl, order?, minTier? })`. |

## PUB Publish to Ruken or itch.io

Owner, 2026-10-16: loosen the Ruken coupling so a game publishes to Ruken or itch.io easily. The [PUB spec](superpowers/specs/2026-10-16-publish-targets-design.md) and [ADR 0005](adr/0005-publish-targets.md) keep Ruken as the reference host and contract vocabulary, and choose the target at build time (`--target ruken|itch|web`). Measured at `cc2ee8c`: `makeHosting` always calls `Ruken.init`, so inside itch.io's iframe the SDK waits 10 s (`HANDSHAKE_TIMEOUT_MS`) before falling back, and its standalone saves silently use memory when storage is blocked. `ship` can only prepare and publish for Ruken.

## QB The quality bar (doesn't look vibecoded)

Owner, 2026-10-16: a checklist "to make sure it doesn't look vibecoded". The [QB spec](superpowers/specs/2026-10-16-quality-bar-design.md) gives each item one disposition: a gate (a check that fails), a task, a request to Ruken (game hosting is Ruken's), or not applicable with the trigger that would reopen it. AF has no database, login, uploads or model calls, so most SaaS and platform items are triggers or Ruken requests. Findings at website `bfeaf91` and `489f170`: the site's MIT claim is ahead of the packages (F-1, DX.11), JPEG files named `.png`, no robots.txt or sitemap, no privacy, terms or contact pages, third-party fonts, no browser tests for the site, and no root README, `SECURITY.md` or dependency audit here.

| Done | Task | Prerequisite and deliverable |
|---|---|---|

| [x] | QB.3j — LCP at most 2.5 s on `/`, `/start`, `/examples` | Website; QB.3h. The spec's LCP target, judged where the baseline is recorded (first the GitHub-hosted runner, Chromium 153; since QB.3k the Owner's Mac), not in the container. Plan first, tests first; **Owner 2026-10-23 08:06Z (bus `2026-10-23-0808-hub-owner-accepts-9-and-135`): QB.3j goes ahead as planned; Owner 15:45Z ("Website #10 is fine", relayed 16:06Z): approved, including the hydration trade.** **Status 2026-10-23 (merged):** `mcthaydt/automata-website` PR #10, merged as `d5b8059` ([plan](https://github.com/mcthaydt/automata-website/blob/main/docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-23-qb3j-lcp-under-2-5-s.md); results in that repo's `docs/2026-10-14-site-redesign-results.md`, "QB.3j on the Owner's Mac"). Three causes. (1) Every page listed 26 to 29 `modulepreload` hints for shared chunks (250 to 275 kB built); Lighthouse's simulated slow link shares its bandwidth with them, so the stylesheet and font waited. Now only the entry and node modules are preloaded (four hints a page, a `handle` hook; `check:site` fails a page that preloads a shared chunk or more than 48 kB). (2) On `/`, Chromium 153 fetches `loading="lazy"` pictures 2,000 px down right after the poster (95 kB charged to the LCP element); a `LazyPicture` component puts a picture in the page when an IntersectionObserver reports it within 400 px, with a `<noscript>` copy. (3) Found on the Mac: its first runs measured `/` 2.57 s, `/start` 2.86 s, `/examples` 3.01 s against 2.1, 2.1 and 1.7 s hosted. I first blamed the shared Mac's load and moved the gate to the best of seven runs; the second Mac run (seven runs identical to 2 ms) refuted that, and the gate is the median of three again. The cause is that Lighthouse's simulation charges the first paint with every request that finished before it, and the M1 finishes SvelteKit's whole hydration chain (about 30 modules, 350 kB) in 70 ms, before its first paint at 90 ms; a slower host paints first. Holding the stylesheet back 400 ms reproduces the Mac in a container. The start script now waits for the first contentful paint (`hydrateAfterFirstPaint`, with a visibility-change and a 3 s fallback; `check:site` fails a page that hydrates at once, and a Chromium browser test requires the module requests to start after the paint; 14 planted changes caught). **Result:** hosted (Chromium 153, median of three, two runs) LCP `/` 2,122 and 2,125 ms (was 3,505), `/start` 2,111 and 2,114, `/examples` 1,749 and 1,861, `/docs` 1,636 and 1,599; the Mac's run 39998718879 of the merged head `4740fc4` (all three jobs green, the browsers job 456 passed and 2 skipped) 1,657, 1,807, 1,504 and 1,353 ms at a host load of 16 on 8 cores, where the old code measured 3,175, 2,857, 3,008 and 2,114 ms on the same Mac. Every page is held to performance 0.9 and LCP 2.5 s (`lighthouserc.json`). **Price (the Owner accepted it):** hydration falls after first paint, so on a slow link pages come alive about 0.4 s later than with the shared-chunk hints (slow 4G, 4x CPU) and 0.03 to 0.12 s later again for waiting on the paint; Lighthouse counts hydration as blocking time (hosted `/` 186 and 211 ms, `/examples` 156 and 125 ms; the Mac 31 and 19.5 ms). **Open:** `mcthaydt/automata-website` PR #12 re-records `lighthouse-baseline.json` from the Mac's run and puts every blocking-time line back at 200 ms (the 300 ms allowance existed for the hosted runner; if the CI ever returns to hosted runners it needs it again). |

Ruken's answer (bus 2046, code reading at `bc469b8`): its host does not focus the game frame on load either, so a player clicks first and the spec's focus stands in for the click. Open: under the synthetic overload that reproduced the failure, 2 of 12 runs failed later at the hold steps (`holding right did not move the runner`, a held jump shot): the sim reads input once a tick, so a starved WebKit that draws no frame during the 100 ms hold never sees the key. The next Mac full e2e decides whether that needs its own task. |

Pass the same timeout (or call `pnpm test:unit --filter …`). - **QB.1 and QB.2 converge on one secret rule list.** Once #38 (QB.1, a gitleaks wrapper in `tooling/repo-hygiene`) and QB.2 both merge, choose one of two paths. Either generate QB.1's gitleaks config from `ship`'s `KEY_RULES` (they already use gitleaks' rule IDs), or test that every `KEY_RULES` ID exists in the gitleaks rule set QB.1 pins.

Their values can't be secret, and a known key format in one is still caught by the key rules. Every other `.env*` value of 8 characters or more still fails the build. No allowlist is needed.
- **PRODUCT.6 kits run `check-style`.** The kit rule (`ui` spec §3.11) must run over the kit sources in Automata Plus when PRODUCT.6 creates them.

Owner, 2026-10-30: the agent merges its own PRs once every check, parity included, is green on the head ("Merge" for PR #186, then "you can merge PR"). CI runs on the Owner's Mac (the self-hosted `af-mac` runners; Owner, 2026-10-30, on a second runner on a Windows PC: "That's too much effort, just use the Mac", so there is no second machine); three-browser parity also runs in the agent's container with `PLAYWRIGHT_BROWSERS_PATH=/opt/pw-local`. ## WEB.PLAY — Live code playground on the website (Owner, 2026-10-30)

Owner, 2026-10-30: "the code editor thing that pixi has for the website? That click a button to see changes crap is useless". The [live playground spec](https://github.com/mcthaydt/automata-website/blob/claude/web-live-playground-spec/docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-30-live-playground-design.md) (website PR #14) gives each one-file example its own page at `/examples/<id>`, running when it opens and restarting 300 ms after an edit (PLAY-D1 as the Owner changed it: no Play button; reduced motion shows a still poster). The framework owns the runtime, frame pages, harness and protocol (WEB.PLAY.1 to .3, private tooling); the site copies them, pinned to `framework.ref`. PLAY-D2 to PLAY-D15 run on their recommended defaults (spec §18), open to the Owner's veto; PLAY-D16 (Ruken's SDK packages under MIT) is RukenApp PR #396.

## GL — A game's source grouped by role (Owner, 2026-10-30)

Owner, 2026-10-30: "I wanted the projects structured like the esper redux project", then "make the restructure when it makes sense to". ## ED Editors (Owner amendment, 2026-10-23)

**Direction (Owner via the Orchestrator, 2026-10-23, corrected 01:20 UTC):** the editors are new work that runs in parallel threads alongside the existing plan. Nothing above is paused or reordered. The 3D part of the framework (GAME-3D and M9 below) comes after the editors are done. The 3D game's content is authored in these editors (Owner, 2026-10-23 14:21 UTC: "use the editors"), so GAME-3D.1 starts once the 3D level editor and the Studio are usable. Two tablet-first web apps share one kit: a **3D level editor** ("LDtk meets the 90s Build Editor", extracted from The Last Light's `editor/` suite at `mcthaydt/PixiJS3D_Experiment_001`) and one **2D level editor** close to LDtk that is **genre-general** (Owner, 2026-10-23 02:31 UTC): the same editor builds a 2D platformer level and a top-down RPG map, and a FloorFifty stage is a profile of it ("build a FloorFifty stage" stays a required acceptance case). Design first: the mockups live in the Figma file [Automata Editors](https://www.figma.com/design/8H4vY5xZj7xIedrvDdwg2d) (page `Kit`, `2D ...`, `3D ...`) and are published as artifacts before UI code starts. Each spec is reviewed in its own PR before its plans. Device test runs (Mac, PC, Linux VM) are never run by the lane: it files a `test-request` on the Orchestrator bus. **Superseded 2026-10-30** (Owner: "no more orchestrator, we're only working on AF"): there is no bus; lanes run the browser checks themselves (Chromium, WebKit and Firefox are installed in the agent's container) and ask the Owner in the session for device passes (the iPad and Pencil, Safari OPFS, the Mac's folder picker).

| [x] | ED3D.7a — Thing edits | ED3D.7 (first part), ED3D.1b2, ED3D.2b. The model half of Things, with no view, tool or inspector: how a thing is written into a level of either format (`ThingAccess` and `LevelProfile.things`: the `af-sector/1` `entities` list; Last Light's spawn, lists and records) and the four edits that put a thing in, move it, set or clear a field and take it out (`thing.add`, `thing.move`, `thing.setField`, `thing.remove`), each one undo step. An edit enforces the rules of the `entities` check in the check's own sentences (an id that is an id and unused, one of a `single` definition, a place inside a sector and outside a hole, values that fit their fields, no height for a thing on the floor, nothing the file's schema would refuse), and a removal is refused while a ref, an `on`, a door, a nav edge or the patrol names the thing. The level editor drops the selection of a thing that is gone. Plan `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-23-ed3d-7a-thing-edits.md` (branch `claude/project-thread-39g5i1-ed3d-7a`, draft PR stacked on #166), implemented and locally checked in four steps. Ticked for implemented and locally checked, as ED3D.2b is; not CI verified, not reviewed, not merged. The Things layer, palette, tools and inspector are ED3D.7b onward and ED3D.5; zones and nav edges as things are ED3D.7c. **Merged (verified 2026-10-30):** #168 (`fa3f2cd`); the "draft" and "not merged" notes above are history. |

## EDS Editor suite (coordinator decision, 2026-10-23 07:41Z)

**Direction (decided 2026-10-23 07:41Z (coordinator), relayed on the Orchestrator bus, `automata/inbox/2026-10-23-0742-hub-editor-suite-audit.md`; reversible, and the Owner can override it):** all the editors beside the level editors go into the plan, because the Owner asked for all of them: Sequence, Tuner, Writer, Studio (the character editor), Schematic, Audio and Feel, with the shell, dialogs and portrait views they share (22 screens). They come after the level editors. The spec (EDS.0) may be written and reviewed while the level editors are built; implementation (EDS.1 onward) starts only when ED2D.10 and ED3D.12 are implemented and locally checked, and does not jump ahead of them. Nothing above is reordered. This is a colleague's decision, not Owner approval of the editors' design. **2026-10-30:** with no coordinator, the order stands (the critical path runs the level editors, then EDS.1, EDS.2 and EDS.9, then GAME-3D.1); EDS.0 may be written now, on the editor specs' defaults; the headless EDS.1a to EDS.1c need only merged kit and EDFF work and may run beside the level editors if the Owner agrees. The suite editors that edit Plus extensions' content (EDS.3, EDS.4b, EDS.5 to EDS.8) wait on M6 in automata-plus (decision 7); EDS.0 decides where an editor for Plus-only content lives.

**Order** is by dependency, then value: the shell and the shared ports first, because every editor stands on them; then each editor as soon as the extension whose content it edits exists (the prerequisites below are the M6 and M3 tasks that build them), cheapest and most reused first; the character editor last, because its screens are being redesigned. The suite editors edit AF's content files through the ports, not The Last Light's TypeScript sources (the 2026-10-23 07:44Z audit of the mockups found the screens still show the latter), and every editor must work on a second game's content as well as The Last Light's Bay scene.

## APP Paid editor apps (Owner, 2026-10-30)

Owner, 2026-10-30: "I kinda want to change our plans for paid - we'll make the editor suite paid for iOS and Android instead as apps; priced like nomad sculp". Then: Plus becomes free; editors free on the web and desktop, paid on iOS and Android; a one-time purchase. Spec: [APP](superpowers/specs/2026-10-30-paid-editor-apps-design.md). The apps ship after EDS.11 on their own release, not in the AF 1.0 launch (APP-D8, open to veto).

## The base 3D game and 3D parity (inside AF 1.0)

Owner roadmap (2026-10-11): 2D first, then 3D. **2026-10-18:** 2.5D is dropped. **2026-10-23 08:45 to 08:48 UTC (Owner, project thread; message ids in the M3 spec's "Owner direction" table):** "Cut the rigged gltf, procedural only for now, make the character action adventure like ocarina of time switch 2 remake, but use an angle like crow country for the 3D"; "Character editor is glorious, but let's add like IK rig so we can manually pose, I think that's also necessary for animation which we may have missed?"; "Go with 24mm focal length for camera". **14:18 to 14:21 UTC:** "25deg tilt, 5m distance is great, we can cut the 45 degree snap for rotation, I think per pixel rotation works well since it's fully 3D now; when I said Zelda, I meant that's our base 3D game like our 2D platformer game - I want to do everything we did for 2D but for 3D with procedural character"; "3D ships 1.0"; "Visually it should match the 2D game too in terms of quality, use the editors". So the Ocarina-style action-adventure is the base 3D game (`games/base-3d`, the counterpart of `games/emberwick`), it is built inside AF 1.0 (not after it, and not in its own repo), the characters are procedural on M3's IK rig, its content is authored in the editors, and the AF 1.0 launch (the core release, M8.9) waits for it and for Pixi 3D's public release (**Owner, 14:49 UTC:** "I forgot, we launch when pixi 3D goes public"; we build on the `@pixi/3d` beta meanwhile, M3R-D18). The agent's reading of "everything we did for 2D" is the parity table in the [M3 spec](superpowers/specs/2026-10-18-m3-3d-characters-design.md) §18 (M3R-D19), which GAME-3D.0 confirms. **3D is free in 1.0 (M3R-D10, M3R-D20):** "3D ships 1.0" answered the question "1.0 or Plus?" (the coordinator's reading, 14:37 UTC), so `render-pixi3d`, `figures` and `games/base-3d` are free and ship with the core 1.0 from this repository; `post-retro`, `pipelines` and `physics` keep their Plus tier. **Owner, 2026-10-30 (decision 7):** `games/base-3d` is free and uses core only, like Emberwick: its rooms, keys, feel and cutscenes are game code, and the M6 extensions are built in Plus; M9 promotes the 3D runtime pieces (controller, camera, figures) as free and the gameplay systems (combat, lock-on, items, dungeon pieces) as Plus. **Decision 2:** plan to completion; the launch waits for Pixi 3D's public release, about 2026-11-13.
